"A verdict that DEPENDS on one of these" — if the verdict doesn't depend on the boundary (because all admissible boundaries give the same answer), then it's not unsettled. OK, I need to make a decision and be consistent. Let me decide the boundary policy ONCE:

**Policy**: The theory treats the system boundary as a declared input. A case that names no boundary leaves that input unstated. But the SILENT rule requires the unstated input to be "the deciding matter." I'll treat the boundary as the deciding matter exactly when the case presents a live question about whether a contribution/process counts as the system's own — i.e., when there is an outside author/contributor whose work is routed through the system, or an explicit boundary statement to apply. When the situation describes the agent performing the act itself with no competing claimant and no boundary question raised, the boundary is not the deciding matter.
